Equivariant Poincaré duality for cyclic groups of prime order and the Nielsen realisation problem
Kaif Hilman, Dominik Kirstein, Christian Kremer

Abstract
In this companion article to [ HKK24 ] , we apply the theory of equivariant Poincaré duality developed there in the special case of cyclic groups C p C_{p} of prime order to remove, in a special case, a technical condition given by Davis–Lück [ DL24 ] in their work on the Nielsen realisation problem for aspherical manifolds. Along the way, we will also give a complete characterisation of C p C_{p} –Poincaré spaces as well as introduce a genuine equivariant refinement of the classical notion of virtual Poincaré duality groups which might be of independent interest.

Introduction

A famous question due to Jakob Nielsen
[
Nie32
]
in geometric topology is the following: can any finite subgroup
G\subset\pi_{0}\mathrm{hAut}(\Sigma_{g})
be lifted to an actual continuous group action on
\Sigma_{g}
, for
\Sigma_{g}
a closed oriented surface of genus
g\geq 0
? This turns out to be possible, with Nielsen settling the case of
G
finite cyclic, and Kerckhoff
[
Ker83
]
the general case.

In high-dimensions, asking for too direct a generalisation of Nielsen’s question inevitably results in wrong statements as there is simply no reason why a general homotopy equivalence
h\colon M\rightarrow M
should be homotopic to any homeomorphism. However, rigidity phenomena in the theory of closed aspherical manifolds - closed connected manifolds with contractible universal covers - give some hope in generalising Nielsen’s and Kerckhoff’s results in this direction. This question may thus fairly be called the “generalised Nielsen realisation problem for aspherical manifolds” and has been investigated quite intensively, see for example
[
RS77
,
DL03
,
BW08
,
Lüc22
,
DL24
]
.
3
3

3

For completeness, we mention here that there is also a large body of work on the Nielsen realisation problem for not necessarily aspherical 4–manifolds, c.f. for instance
[
FL24
,
BK23
,
Lee23
,
Kon24
]
, which has a much more geometric flavour.

Unfortunately, even the hypothesis of closed aspherical manifolds is not quite adequate, and we refer to
[
Wei23
]
for a delightful survey of counterexamples.
Nevertheless, it turns out to be quite easy to dodge all potential reasons for counterexamples (for example, the failure for the existence of necessary group extensions due to Raymond–Scott
[
RS77
]
) by asking a slight variation of the generalised Nielsen problem:

Question 1.1
.

Let
M
be an aspherical manifold with fundamental group
\pi
and consider an extension of groups

1\rightarrow\pi\rightarrow\Gamma\rightarrow G\rightarrow 1

where
G
is finite of odd order
4
4

4

Taking
G
to be of odd order implies that certain
\mathrm{UNil}
-valued obstructions vanish, see
[
DL24
, Thm. 1.16.]
or
[
Wei23
, Sec. 6.4.]
.
.
Does the
\pi
-action on the universal cover
\widetilde{M}
of
M
extend to a
\Gamma
-action such that

\widetilde{M}^{H}\simeq\begin{cases}*\hskip 5.69054pt\text{if $H\leq\Gamma$, and $H$ is finite};\\
\emptyset\hskip 5.69054pt\text{if $H\leq\Gamma$, and $H$ is infinite}?\end{cases}

Equivalently, does the
\pi
-action on the universal cover of
M
extend to a
\Gamma
-action in a way such that the resulting
\Gamma
-space models
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
, the universal space for proper
\Gamma
-actions?

Provided the answer to
Question

1.1
is yes, one may construct a
G
-action on
M
by using the residual action on
\pi\backslash\widetilde{M}
. For an account of the relation of
Question

1.1
to the generalized Nielsen realization problem in terms of homomorphisms
G\rightarrow\pi_{0}(\mathrm{hAut}(M))\cong\mathrm{Out}(\pi_{1}(M))
, we refer the reader to the introduction of
[
DL24
]
.

In this article, we give a positive answer to
Question

1.1
in the very special situation when
M
is high-dimensional,
\pi
is hyperbolic,
G=C_{p}
for
p
odd, and if the extension is what we call
pseudofree
, i.e. if each nontrivial finite subgroup
F\subset\Gamma
satisfies
N_{\Gamma}F=F
.
Geometrically, this predicts that any
\Gamma
–manifold model
\widetilde{M}
must have discrete fixed points (see
Remark

4.1.5
), whence the name. One of our main results is the following:

Theorem A
.

Consider a group extension

1\rightarrow\pi\rightarrow\Gamma\rightarrow C_{p}\rightarrow 1

(1)

for an odd prime p. Suppose that

(1)

\pi=\pi_{1}(M)
for a closed orientable
5
5

5

Orientability is assumed only to simplify the exposition, and can be removed with some care.
aspherical manifold
M
of dimension at least 5,

(2)

\pi
is hyperbolic,

(3)

\Gamma
is pseudofree.

Then there exists a cocompact

\Gamma
-manifold model for
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
.

To the best of our knowledge, the most general existence result for manifold models for
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
that does not refer to specific differential geometric constructions is due to Davis-Lück
[
DL24
, Thm. 1.16]
, whose methods are mainly surgery– and K–theoretic.
They prove
Theorem

A
under an additional necessary group homological “Condition (H)” (c.f.
Condition

1.3
) on
\Gamma
which is previously considered mysterious.
This Condition (H) was discovered by Lück in
[
Lüc22
]
as necessary for the existence of manifold models for
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
, but was also used to construct certain models for
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
which satisfy some kind of equivariant Poincaré duality.
Davis-Lück then show in which situations these can actually be turned into equivariant manifolds.
Condition (H), however, seems complicated and hard to verify.
Our main contribution to this problem is to show that, in the situation of
Theorem

A
, Condition (H) is actually automatic, and we achieve this by locating it in the more conceptual context of
equivariant Poincaré duality
as developed in
[
HKK24
]
. We hope that these techniques will allow us to go beyond the pseudofree situation where Davis-Lück applies, so that in the future we might be able to construct group actions on aspherical manifolds with nondiscrete fixed point sets. As will be clear later, our main input to remove Davis-Lück’s Condition (H),
Theorem

C
, does not refer to discrete fixed points at all.

To round off our commentary on
Theorem

A
, it should also be noted that, in principle, the theorem reduces the geometric problem to a purely algebraic one of producing the appropriate group extension under the given hypotheses on
p
and
\pi
.
As explained e.g. in
[
DL24
, p.1]
, there is an obstruction measuring when a homomorphism
C_{p}\to\out(\pi)
is induced by an extension
Eq.

1
.
It vanishes for hyperbolic groups as they have trivial center.
For the Nielsen realisation problem,
Theorem

A
thus has the following implication.

Corollary 1.2
.

Let
M
be a closed orientable aspherical manifold with hyperbolic fundamental group of dimension at least
5
,
p
an odd prime, and
\alpha\colon C_{p}\rightarrow\out(\pi_{1}M)
a homomorphism. Then the Nielsen realisation problem for
\alpha
admits a solution, provided the associated extension
\Gamma
is pseudofree.

Before moving on to elaborate on equivariant Poincaré duality as used in this work, we first state the aforementioned Condition (H) and recall the argument of
[
Lüc22
, Lemma 1.9]
as to why it is necessary for the conclusion of
Theorem

A
to hold. This shows that Condition (H) is not merely an artefact of the proof strategy of
[
DL24
]
but is rather a point that must be dealt with in one way or another.

Eq.

2
, a result of Lück–Weiermann
[
LW12
]
(c.f.
Theorem

4.1.4
) shows that the subspace

d
-dimensional cocompact manifold model
N
for

.
Let us assume for simplicity that
N
is smooth and that
\Gamma
acts smoothly preserving the orientation.
As mentioned before, the singular part
N^{>1}
of
N
of points with nontrivial isotropy is discrete if the extension is pseudofree.
Denote by
Q
the complement of an equivariant tubular neighbourhood of
N^{>1}
in
N
with boundary
\partial Q
.
Then the
\Gamma
-action on
Q
is free and the quotient pair
(\Gamma\backslash Q,\Gamma\backslash\partial Q)
is a compact
d
-manifold with boundary. See
Fig.

2
for an illustration.

L
of
\Gamma\backslash\partial Q
, we obtain the commutative diagram

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{proj}}

The left vertical arrow is an equivalence using excision and that homology of
(\Gamma\backslash Q,\Gamma\backslash\partial Q)
agrees with Borel homology of
(Q,\partial Q)
as the
\Gamma
-action is free.
The fundamental class of
(\Gamma\backslash Q,\Gamma\backslash\partial Q)
gets sent to a fundamental class of each boundary component along the upper composite, and so the top composite is surjective. Moreover, note that each component
L
of
\Gamma\backslash\partial Q
is obtained as the quotient of a sphere by a free action of the isotropy group
F
of the corresponding fixed point in
N^{>1}
.
Recall that for any free
F
-action on a
(d-1)
–sphere
S
for a finite group
F
, the map
F\backslash S\rightarrow BF
is is
(d-1)
–connected and induces a surjection on homology up to degree
d-1
so the right vertical map is surjective.
Together this shows that the bottom composite is surjective in each component.

Figure 2:
Discs around the singular part for the symmetry group p3

1.1
Equivariant Poincaré duality

Equivariant Poincaré duality is fundamentally about understanding group actions on manifolds. The notion of a
G
-equivariant Poincaré complex is designed to satisfy more or less all the homological or cohomological constraints that a smooth
G
-manifold satisfies. In particular, satisfying equivariant Poincaré duality can
obstruct
the existence of certain group actions on manifolds. This philosophy is old and has been quite successful, and we exploited it in
[
HKK24
]
to generalise some classical nonexistence results
6
6

6

See also the references therein for more results of this type.
using new methods.

In this article, we want to carry across a different point: equivariant Poincaré duality does not only obstruct, but is also quite useful to
construct
group actions on manifolds. The testing ground we chose to demonstrate our claim is
Question

1.1
. Here, we will only employ the theory of equivariant Poincaré duality for the group
G=C_{p}
, and since this case is much simpler than for general compact Lie groups, we hope that it will demystify the more abstract discussions in
[
HKK24
]
. In particular, we can keep the level of equivariant stable homotopy theory used throughout at a minimum, while still showing some standard manipulations. We hope that readers with an interest in geometric topology and homotopy theory might find this to be a useful first exposition to categories of genuine
G
-spectra and their uses.

Equivariant Poincaré duality for the group
C_{p}

Our first theoretical goal is to give a characterisation of
C_{p}
-Poincaré duality that is adapted to our application on the Nielsen realisation problem.
In
[
HKK24
]
we showed that if
\underline{{X}}
is a
C_{p}
-space, then the following hold:

(1)

If
\underline{{X}}
is
C_{p}
-Poincaré, then the underlying space
X^{e}
and the fixed points
X^{C_{p}}
are nonequivariantly Poincaré (c.f.
[
HKK24
, Thm. C]
).

(2)

Even if
\underline{{X}}
is assumed to be a compact (i.e. equivariantly finitely dominated)
C_{p}
-space, requiring
X^{e}
and
X^{C_{p}}
to be nonequivariantly Poincaré is not sufficient to guarantee that
\underline{{X}}
is
C_{p}
-Poincaré (c.f.
[
HKK24
, Cor. 5.1.16]
).

In this article, we identify the precise additional condition needed to ensure that
\underline{{X}}
is
C_{p}
–Poincaré in the situation of (2).

Theorem B

(c.f.
Theorem

3.3.3
)
.

Let
\underline{{X}}
be a compact
C_{p}
-space.
Denote by
\varepsilon\colon X^{C_{p}}\rightarrow X^{e}
the inclusion of the fixed points, and assume that both
X^{C_{p}}
and
X^{e}
are nonequivariantly Poincaré. Let
D_{X^{C_{p}}}\in\func(X^{C_{p}},\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}})
be the dualising sheaf of the fixed point space. Then
\underline{{X}}
is
C_{p}
-Poincaré if and only if the cofibre of the adjunction unit morphism in
\func(X^{C_{p}},\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}})

D_{X^{C_{p}}}\rightarrow\varepsilon^{*}\varepsilon_{!}D_{X^{C_{p}}}

pointwise lies in
\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}}_{\mathrm{Ind}}\subseteq\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}}
, the stable full subcategory generated by the image of
\mathrm{Ind}^{C_{p}}_{e}\colon\mathrm{Sp}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}}
.

More details on the terms appearing in the theorem may be found in the material leading up to
Theorem

3.3.3
. In effect, this result gives an interpretation of the genuine equivariant notion of Poincaré duality purely in terms of nonequivariant and Borel equivariant properties. The method of proof is based on various cellular manoeuvres in equivariant stable homotopy theory developed in
Section

3
, which might be of independent interest. Armed with this characterisation, we may now return to the modified Nielsen
Question

1.1
as we explain next.

E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}$}\hss}\mbox{$\scriptscriptstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}$}}}}
is a
C_{p}
-Poincaré space. In fact, we define the notion of a genuine virtual Poincaré duality group in a broader context in
Section

4
and we hope that it can be a useful supplement to the classical theory of virtual Poincaré duality groups that appear for example in
[
Bro94
]
. In any case, using
Theorem

B
, we will show our main result:

Theorem C

(c.f.
Theorem

5.1.4
)
.

For the extension
Eq.

2
, assume that
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
\textstyle E_{\Gamma}\mathrm{Fin}
is compact. If

(1)

for each nontrivial finite subgroup
F\subset\Gamma
, the group
W_{\Gamma}F
is a Poincaré duality group;

(2)

the group
\pi
is a Poincaré duality group,

then
\Gamma
is a genuine virtual Poincaré duality group.

The significance of this result is that we relate the new notion of genuine virtual Poincaré duality groups, which enjoys good conceptual properties, with the classical notion of Poincaré duality groups, which is easier to check. It will turn out that, in the situation of
Theorem

A
, the group
\Gamma
satisfies the conditions of
Theorem

, and so we see that

, §4.5]
) on the problem at hand, yielding the following:

Theorem D
.

Let
\Gamma
be as in
Theorem

A
.
Then
\Gamma
satisfies Condition (H).

A proof of this, and the more general
Proposition

, will be given in

. Taking this for granted for the moment, we may now provide the proof of
Theorem

A
.

Proof of
Theorem

A
.

This is now a direct consequence of
[
DL24
, Theorem 1.4]
.
The group theoretic assumptions therein are satisfied by

5.1.1
, whereas Condition (H) is shown to hold in

.
∎

Structural overview

We recall in
Section

2
some notions and constructions from the theory of equivariant Poincaré duality
[
HKK24
]
that will be pertinent to our purposes. Next, in
Section

3
, we work towards proving
Theorem

B
, and for this, it will be necessary to develop some theory on compact objects in
C_{p}
–genuine spectra. This we do in
Sections

3.1
and
3.2
, which might be of independent interest. Having set up the requisite basic theory, we return to the problem at hand and define the notion of genuine virtual Poincaré duality groups in
Section

4
, refining the classical notion of virtual Poincaré duality groups. Therein, we will also prove a characterisation result tailored to our needs. Finally, we put together all the elements and prove
C
and
D
in
Section

5
.

Conventions

This paper is written in the language of
\infty
–categories as set down in
[
Lur09
,
Lur17
]
, and so by a
category
we will always mean an
\infty
–category unless stated otherwise.

2
Recollections

There will be two types of equivariance in this paper, each playing a distinct role. The first kind will be defined for an arbitrary Lie group, which is covered in
Section

2.1
; the second kind, covered in
Section

2.2
, will be defined only for finite groups (in fact, it is defined more generally for compact Lie groups as in
[
HKK24
]
) and is the one that supports stable homotopy theory and the theory of equivariant Poincaré duality in
Section

2.3
. More details on the materials in
Sections

2.2
and
2.3
together with references to the original sources may be found in
[
HKK24
]
.

2.1
Equivariant spaces

Throughout, let
\Gamma
be a Lie group.

Notation 2.1.1
.

Let
\mathcal{O}(\Gamma)
be the topological category of homogeneous
\Gamma
–spaces, the full topological subcategory of the category of topological
\Gamma
-spaces
on objects isomorphic to
\Gamma/H
for closed subgroups
H\leq\Gamma
.

Definition 2.1.2
.

The category
\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}
of

\Gamma
–spaces
is defined as the category of presheaves
\presheaf(\mathcal{O}(\Gamma))\coloneqq\func(\mathcal{O}(\Gamma)^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{S})
.

Construction 2.1.3

(Fundamental adjunctions)
.

Genuine equivariant spaces participate in many adjunctions, the fundamental one that we will need being the following: let
\alpha\colon K\rightarrow\Gamma
be a continuous homomorphism of Lie groups.
By left Kan extension and restriction along the (opposite) induction functor
\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}\colon\mathcal{O}(K)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathcal{O}(\Gamma)^{\mathrm{op}}
, we obtain the adjunction
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ind}_{\alpha}\coloneqq(\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})_{!}\colon\mathcal{S}_{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 2.0mu\res_{\alpha}\coloneqq(\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})^{*}.}

Specialising to the two cases of
\alpha=\iota\colon H\rightarrowtail\Gamma
being a closed subgroup and
\alpha=\theta\colon\Gamma\twoheadrightarrow Q
being a continuous surjection of Lie groups with kernel
N
, the adjunction above yields the following two adjunctions which we have given special notations
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ind}^{\Gamma}_{H}\coloneqq\mathrm{Ind}_{\iota}\colon\mathcal{S}_{H}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 2.0mu\res^{\Gamma}_{H}\coloneqq\res_{\iota}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N\backslash(-)\coloneqq\mathrm{Ind}_{\theta}\colon\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{Q}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 2.0mu\mathrm{infl}^{Q}_{\Gamma}\coloneqq\res_{\theta}.}

Importantly, in the special case of a continuous surjection
\theta\colon\Gamma\twoheadrightarrow Q
, we have an adjunction
\res^{\mathcal{O}}_{\theta}\colon\mathcal{O}(Q)^{\mathrm{op}}\rightleftharpoons\mathcal{O}(\Gamma)^{\mathrm{op}}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 2.0mu\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathcal{O}}_{\theta}
, and so
\infl^{Q}_{\Gamma}=(\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathcal{O}}_{\theta})^{*}\simeq(\res^{\mathcal{O}}_{\theta})_{!}
.

Observation 2.1.4
.

In particular, suppose we have homomorphisms of Lie groups
\iota\colon N\rightarrowtail\Gamma
and
\theta\colon\Gamma\twoheadrightarrow Q
which are injective and surjective, respectively, and such that the composite
\theta\circ\iota\colon N\rightarrow Q
is also surjective. Writing
\pi
for the kernel of
\theta
, we thus see that
\ker(\theta\circ\iota)=N\cap\pi
. Since for composable homomorphisms of Lie groups
\alpha
and
\beta
we have
\mathrm{Ind}_{\alpha\circ\beta}\simeq\mathrm{Ind}_{\alpha}\circ\mathrm{Ind}_{\beta}
, we see that in this case, there is a natural equivalence of functors
\mathcal{S}_{N}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}_{Q}

\pi\backslash\mathrm{Ind}^{\Gamma}_{N}(-)\simeq(N\cap\pi)\backslash(-).

Construction 2.1.5

(Singular parts)
.

Denote by
s\colon\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\Gamma)\subseteq\mathcal{O}(\Gamma)
the full subcategory on the orbits
\Gamma/H
with
H
nontrivial. We then get the adjunction
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\presheaf(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\Gamma))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\presheaf(\mathcal{O}(\Gamma))=\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{*}}

by restricting and left Kan extending along
s
. We abbreviate
{(-)}^{>1}=s_{!}s^{*}
, writing
\varepsilon\colon{\underline{{X}}}^{>1}\rightarrow\underline{{X}}
for the adjunction counit. Note that for an orbit
\mathchoice{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\displaystyle\Gamma/H$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\mbox{$\scriptstyle\Gamma/H$}}}}{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma/H$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma/H$}\hss}\mbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma/H$}}}}
is an equivalence otherwise.
We refer to
{\underline{{X}}}^{>1}
as the
singular part of
\underline{{X}}
and think of
\varepsilon\colon{\underline{{X}}}^{>1}\rightarrow\underline{{X}}
as the inclusion of all points with nontrivial isotropy.

Notation 2.1.6
.

In the special case of
\Gamma=C_{p}
, we have
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sing}}(C_{p})=\{C_{p}/C_{p}\}\simeq\ast
, so that
\presheaf(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sing}}(C_{p}))\simeq\mathcal{S}
. In this case, one can work out that
s_{!}
just assigns a space to the constant diagram as an object in
\mathcal{S}_{C_{p}}
. Moreover,
s^{*}\underline{{X}}=X^{C_{p}}
, and so we will also write
{\underline{{X}}}^{>1}\in\mathcal{S}_{C_{p}}
as
\underline{{X^{C_{p}}}}
interchangeably in this case.

Fact 2.1.7
.

Let
\Gamma
and
Q
be groups,
N\leq\Gamma
a normal subgroup and
p\colon\Gamma\rightarrow Q
a surjective group homomorphism.
If
Q
acts on the topological space
X
, then there is a natural
\Gamma/N
-equivariant homeomorphism

N\backslash X\cong p(N)\backslash X.

Here,
X
is considered as a
\Gamma
-space via
p
, and
p(N)\backslash X
is considered as a
\Gamma/N
-space via
\Gamma/N\rightarrow Q/p(N)
. Specialising to orbit categories, we obtain the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}(Q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}(\Gamma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}(Q/p(N))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}(\Gamma/N).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\res^{\mathcal{O}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathcal{O}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathcal{O}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\res^{\mathcal{O}}}

Applying
\presheaf(-)
with the
(-)_{!}
functoriality, we get an equivalence of functors

N\backslash\infl_{\Gamma}^{Q}(-)\simeq\infl_{\Gamma/N}^{Q/p(N)}p(N)\backslash(-)\colon\mathcal{S}_{Q}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma/N}.

Fact 2.1.8
.

Given any discrete group
\Gamma
and a
\Gamma
–space
\underline{{X}}
, as well as a
proper
normal subgroup
N\subset\Gamma
such that the
N
-action on
\underline{{X}}
is free, the inclusion
\underline{{X}}^{>1}\rightarrow\underline{{X}}
induces equivalences

(N\backslash\underline{{X}})^{>1}\xleftarrow{\simeq}(N\backslash\underline{{X}}^{>1})^{>1}\xrightarrow{\simeq}N\backslash\underline{{X}}^{>1}.

Indeed, all functors involved commute with colimits, and the statement is clearly true for orbits, out of which every
\Gamma
–space may be built via colimits.

2.2
Genuine equivariant stable homotopy theory

Let
G
be a finite group. The stable category

\mathrm{Sp}_{G}
of genuine
G
–spectra
is a refinement of the category
\mathrm{Sp}^{BG}
of spectra with
G
–action with better formal properties. This is a refinement in that
\mathrm{Sp}^{BG}
sits fully faithfully in the category
\mathrm{Sp}_{G}
, in fact in two different ways. One way to define
\mathrm{Sp}_{G}
, following Barwick, is as the category
\mackey_{G}(\mathrm{Sp})\coloneqq\func^{\times}(\spancategory(\mathrm{Fin}_{G}),\mathrm{Sp})
of
G
–Mackey functors in spectra. A good introduction to the materials in this subsection may be found, for instance, in
[
MNN17
, Part 2]
.

The category of genuine equivariant spectra is valuable as it is a particularly conducive environment for inductive methods enabled by many compatibility structures between these categories for different groups, expressed in terms of various adjunctions. Moreover,
\mathrm{Sp}_{G}
should also be thought of as the “universal category of equivariant homology theories” on
\mathcal{S}_{G}
. For example, there is a symmetric monoidal colimit–preserving functor
{\Sigma}^{\infty}_{+}\colon\mathcal{S}_{G}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}_{G}
which is the analogue of the suspension spectrum functor nonequivariant spectra, and
\mathrm{Sp}_{G}
is generated under colimits by
\{{\Sigma}^{\infty}_{+}\mathchoice{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\displaystyle G/H$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle G/H$}\hss}\mbox{$\displaystyle G/H$}}}}{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\textstyle G/H$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle G/H$}\hss}\mbox{$\textstyle G/H$}}}}{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\scriptstyle G/H$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\mbox{$\scriptstyle G/H$}}}}{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\mbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}}}}\}_{H\leq G}
.

Fact 2.2.1

(Restriction–(co)induction)
.

For a subgroup
H\leq G
, we have the adjunctions
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sp}_{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sp}_{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\res^{G}_{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ind}^{G}_{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coind^{G}_{H}}

where moreover, there is a canonical equivalence of functors
\mathrm{Ind}^{G}_{H}\simeq\coind^{G}_{H}
classically known as the Wirthmüller isomorphism.

Fact 2.2.2

(Genuine fixed points)
.

There is a functor
(-)^{G}\colon\mathrm{Sp}_{G}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}
called the
genuine fixed points functor
which, from the Mackey functors perspective, is given by evaluating at
G/G\in\mathrm{Fin}_{G}
. This participates in an adjunction
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{infl}^{e}_{G}\colon\mathrm{Sp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sp}_{G}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 2.0mu(-)^{G}}

where
\mathrm{infl}^{e}_{G}
preserves compact objects and is the unique symmetric monoidal colimit preserving functor from
\mathrm{Sp}
to
\mathrm{Sp}_{G}
. For every subgroup
H\leq G
, we may also define the genuine
H
–fixed points functor
(-)^{H}
as the composite
\mathrm{Sp}_{G}\xrightarrow{\res^{G}_{H}}\mathrm{Sp}_{H}\xrightarrow{(-)^{H}}\mathrm{Sp}
.

Fact 2.2.3

(Borel fixed points)
.

There is a standard Bousfield (co)localisation
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sp}_{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sp}^{BG}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{*}}

where
\beta^{*}X\simeq X^{e}
,
\beta_{!}\beta^{*}X\simeq EG_{+}\otimes X
, and
\beta_{*}\beta^{*}X\simeq F(EG_{+},X)
. This well–known pair of adjunctions may for example be worked out from combining
[
MNN17
, Thm. 3.9, Prop. 6.5, Prop. 6.6, Prop. 6.17]
. Under the Mackey functors perspective,
\beta^{*}
is given by evaluating at
G/e\in\mathrm{Fin}_{G}
. In particular, we see that
\mathrm{Sp}^{BG}
embeds into
\mathrm{Sp}_{G}
in two different ways, as mentioned above. Via the functor
\beta^{*}
as well as the homotopy orbits
(-)_{hG}
, homotopy fixed points
(-)^{hG}
, and Tate fixed points
(-)^{tG}
functors
\mathrm{Sp}^{BG}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}
, we may also obtain the functors
(-)_{hG},(-)^{hG},(-)^{tG}\colon\mathrm{Sp}_{G}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}
which also fit in a fibre sequence of functors
(-)_{hG}\rightarrow(-)^{hG}\rightarrow(-)^{tG}
. In particular, these functors only depend on the underlying spectrum with
G
–action.

Fact 2.2.4

(Geometric fixed points)
.

There is a symmetric monoidal colimit–preserving functor
\Phi^{G}(-)\colon\mathrm{Sp}_{G}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}
called the
geometric fixed points
which is uniquely characterised by sending
{\Sigma}^{\infty}_{+}\mathchoice{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\displaystyle G/H$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle G/H$}\hss}\mbox{$\displaystyle G/H$}}}}{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\textstyle G/H$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox 0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}\hss}\mbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G/H$}}}}
to
0
when
H\lneq G
and to
\mathbb{S}
when
H=G
. For a subgroup
H\leq G
, we may also define
\Phi^{H}
as the functor
\mathrm{Sp}_{G}\xrightarrow{\res^{G}_{H}}\mathrm{Sp}_{H}\xrightarrow{\Phi^{H}}\mathrm{Sp}
. The collection of functors
\Phi^{H}\colon\mathrm{Sp}_{G}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}
for all
H\leq G
is jointly conservative.

The geometric fixed points functor participates in an adjunction
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{G}\colon\mathrm{Sp}_{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sp}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 2.0mu\Xi^{G}}

where
\Xi^{G}
is fully faithful. For
E\in\mathrm{Sp}
,
\Xi^{G}E\in\mathrm{Sp}_{G}
is concretely given by the
G
–Mackey functor which assigns
E
to
G/G
and
0
to all
G/H
for
H\lneq G
.

Furthermore, using that
\mathrm{Sp}
is the initial presentably symmetric monoidal stable category, it is also not hard to see that
\Phi^{G}\mathrm{infl}^{e}_{G}\simeq\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{Sp}}
.

Next, we recall the standard decomposition in the special case of genuine
C_{p}
–spectra, which is all that we will need in our work.

Fact 2.2.5

(
C_{p}
–stable recollement)
.

Let
G=C_{p}
. In this case, some of the adjunctions we have seen fit into a stable recollement (also called split Verdier sequence)
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sp}_{C_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Xi^{C_{p}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{C_{p}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{*}}

in that the top two layers of composites are fibre–cofibre sequences of presentable stable categories. This may be deduced, for example, from a combination of
[
MNN17
, §6.4]
and
[
CDH+23a
, §A.2]
. From this, one obtains for every
E\in\mathrm{Sp}_{C_{p}}
a pullback square
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{C_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{C_{p}}E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{hC_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{tC_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

of spectra (c.f. for instance
[
MNN17
, Thm. 6.24]
or
[
CDH+23a
, Prop. A.2.12]
).

2.3
Equivariant Poincaré duality

Let
G
be a finite group.
We briefly recall the theory of
G
-equivariant Poincaré duality spaces, which is built upon the notion of
G
–categories. Recall that the category
\mathrm{Cat}_{G}
of
G
–categories is defined as
\func(\mathcal{O}(G)^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Cat})
, akin to the category of
G
–spaces. This category admits an internal functor category
\underline{{\func}}(\underline{{}},\underline{{}})
for each pair
\underline{{}},\underline{{}}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{G}
. This satisfies

\underline{{\func}}(\underline{{}},\underline{{}})(G/H)\simeq\func_{H}(\res^{G}_{H}\underline{{}},\res^{G}_{H}\underline{{}}),

where the latter is the category of
H
–functors from
\res^{G}_{H}\underline{{}}
to
\res^{G}_{H}\underline{{}}
. A very important
G
-category for us is the
G
-category

for the category of equivariant local systems on

. Explicitly, that amounts to specifying a local system of
H
-spectra

for each subgroup
H\subset G
plus compatibilities.
Colimit, restriction and limit of local systems give two adjunctions

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{*}}

One should think of the colimit
X_{!}E
of an equivariant local system
E
on
\underline{{X}}
as equivariant homology of
X
twisted by
E
and similarly of the limit as equivariant twisted cohomology.

The following is a recollection from
[
HKK24
, Sec. 4.1.]
.
A compact
G
-space
\underline{{X}}
admits an equivariant dualising spectrum
D_{\underline{{X}}}\in\func_{G}(\underline{{X}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip which comes together with a collapse map
c\colon\mathbb{S}_{G}\rightarrow X_{!}D_{\underline{{X}}}
.
These are uniquely characterised by the property that the induced capping map

{{c}\cap_{\xi}{(-)}}\colon X_{*}(-)\to X_{!}(D_{\underline{{X}}}\otimes-)

(3)

is an equivalence.
Applying fixed points and homotopy groups, the collapse map really corresponds to a class in twisted equivariant homology such that capping with it induces an equivalence between equivariant cohomology and twisted equivariant homology.
Let us just mention that there is the larger class of
twisted ambidextrous

G
-spaces for which an equivalence of the form
Eq.

3
exists.

.

-space
\underline{{X}}

G
-Poincaré

to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\mbox{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}}}{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\mbox{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}}}{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\mbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}}})\simeq\mathrm{Sp}_{H}

we obtain an
H
-spectrum that we will denote by
\xi(y)
.

Note that a compact (or twisted ambidextrous)
G
-space
\underline{{X}}
is
G
-Poincaré if and only if for each
y\in X^{H}
the value
D_{\underline{{X}}}(y)\in\mathrm{Sp}_{H}
is an invertible
H
-spectrum.

Theorem 2.3.3

(
[
HKK24
, Thm 4.2.9.]
)
.

Let
\underline{{X}}
be a
G
-Poincaré space. Then for each closed subgroup
H\leq G
, the space
X^{H}
is a (nonequivariant) Poincaré space. Moreover, its dualising spectrum is given as the composite

X^{H}\xrightarrow{D_{\underline{{X}}}}\mathrm{Sp}_{H}\xrightarrow{\Phi^{H}}\mathrm{Sp}.

Example 2.3.4

(
[
HKK24
, Cor. 5.1.16.]
)
.

Let
p
be an odd prime and
k\geq 1
an integer.
There exists a compact
C_{p}
-space
\underline{{X}}
for which

X^{e}
is contractible while
X^{C_{p}}\simeq\mathbb{R}P^{2k}
. None such
C_{p}
-space is
C_{p}
-Poincaré.
In particular, there are compact
G
-spaces such that all fixed points are nonequivariant Poincaré spaces which are not themselves
G
-Poincaré.

3
Poincaré duality for the group
C_{p}

In this section, we investigate equivariant Poincaré duality for the group
C_{p}
more closely.
Our goal is to prove
Theorem

B
(c.f.
Theorem

3.3.3
) which gives a somewhat computable condition on a
compact

C_{p}
-space
\underline{{X}}
to be
C_{p}
-Poincaré assuming that
X^{e}
and
X^{C_{p}}
are nonequivariant Poincaré spaces. This amounts to checking that
D_{\underline{{X}}}\colon\underline{{X}}\rightarrow\mathchoice{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\mbox{$\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}}}{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\mbox{$\textstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}}}{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\mbox{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}}}{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\mbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}}}}
lands in invertible objects.

Since invertibility is a pointwise condition and since we already know that
D_{X^{e}}\colon X^{e}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}
lands in invertible objects, it suffices to show that
D_{\underline{{X}}}(x)\in\mathrm{Sp}_{C_{p}}
is invertible for every
x\in X^{C_{p}}
. Moreover, from our hypothesis and
Theorem

2.3.3
, we already know that
D_{\underline{{X}}}(x)^{e}
and
\Phi^{C_{p}}D_{\underline{{X}}}(x)
are invertible spectra. This consideration leads us to record the following well–known observation.

Lemma 3.0.1
.

Let
E\in\mathrm{Sp}_{C_{p}}
be such that
E^{e}
and
\Phi^{C_{p}}E
are invertible. Then:

\text{$E$ is invertible}\hskip 8.53581pt\iff\hskip 8.53581pt\text{$E$ is dualisable}\hskip 8.53581pt\iff\hskip 8.53581pt\text{$E$ is compact}.

Proof.

In
\mathrm{Sp}_{C_{p}}
, dualisablity and compactness are equivalent, and invertible spectra are dualisable.
So it suffices to show that if
E
is dualisable, then it is invertible, i.e. that the counit
E\otimes E^{\vee}\rightarrow\mathbb{S}_{C_{p}}

is an equivalence.
But this can be checked after applying
(-)^{e}
and
\Phi^{C_{p}}(-)
, which are jointly conservative.
As both of these functors are symmetric monoidal, the counit for
E
is sent to the counit for
E^{e}
and
\Phi^{C_{p}}E
, both of which we assumed to be equivalences.
∎

Thus, by virtue of
Lemma

3.0.1
, our task at hand is tantamount to ensuring that the
C_{p}
-spectrum
D_{\underline{{X}}}(x)
is compact for every
x\in X^{C_{p}}
. To this end, we will employ various cellular manoeuvres in
Section

3.1
to obtain “compact approximations” to any
C_{p}
–spectrum; we then characterise compactness of a
C_{p}
–spectrum with vanishing geometric fixed points through its underlying Borel-
C_{p}
-spectrum in
Section

3.2
. Lastly, we combine all these in
Section

3.3
to obtain the promised recognition principle for
C_{p}
-Poincaré spaces.

Remark 3.0.2
.

Our work on
C_{p}
-spectra heavily drew inspiration from at least two sources. The first one being
[
Kra20
]
, which gives a nice computation of the Picard group of
\mathrm{Sp}_{C_{p}}
, and whose methods we expand on. The second one is
[
KMS21
]
, which gives another compactness (or dualisability) criterion for
C_{p}
-spectra. Our approach is not exactly tailored to the methods in the latter source, and we will not need to refer to them, but it might be possible that they give another way of proving the main results in this section.

3.1
Cellular manoeuvres and compact approximations

Recall that a
C_{p}
-spectrum is
finite
if it lies in the stable subcategory of
\mathrm{Sp}_{C_{p}}
generated by

C_{p}/e$}}}}{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\scriptstyle C_{p}/e$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle C_{p}/e$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle C_{p}/e$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle and
\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}\mathchoice{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\displaystyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\displaystyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\mbox{$\displaystyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}}}}{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\textstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\mbox{$\textstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}}}}{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\scriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\mbox{$\scriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}}}}{\mbox{\uline{\phantom{$\scriptscriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{-0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{-0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{-0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.19133pt\raisebox{0.46193pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}\hss}\mbox{$\scriptscriptstyle C_{p}/C_{p}$}}}}=\infl_{e}^{C_{p}}\mathbb{S}
.
A
C_{p}
-spectrum is compact if and only if it is a retract of a finite
C_{p}
-spectrum.

Lemma 3.1.1

(Tucking the free part)
.

Let
X\in\mathrm{Sp}_{C_{p}}
be such that
X^{e}
is bounded below and such that
\pi_{k}(X^{e})
is a finitely generated abelian group for
k\leq N
for some
N
. Then there is a fiber sequence

F\rightarrow X\rightarrow Y

in
\mathrm{Sp}_{C_{p}}
such that
F
is finite,
Y^{e}
is
N
-connected and
\Phi^{C_{p}}X\rightarrow\Phi^{C_{p}}Y
is an equivalence.

Proof.

Note that if
A\rightarrow B
and
B\rightarrow C
are maps of
C_{p}
-spectra whose fibers are compact with trivial geometric fixed points, then the composition
A\rightarrow C
satisfies the same condition. Thus, by induction it suffices to consider the case where
N=0
and
X^{e}
is
-1
-connected. Pick a finite set of generators
\{f_{i}\colon\mathbb{S}\rightarrow X^{e}\}
of
\pi_{0}(X^{e})
. Then the composition

f=\left(\bigoplus_{i}\mathrm{Ind}\mathbb{S}\xrightarrow{\bigoplus_{i}\mathrm{Ind}f_{i}}\mathrm{Ind}X^{e}\xrightarrow{c}X\right),

where
c
denotes the counit, induces a surjection on
\pi_{0}
upon applying
(-)^{e}
. Now define
Y
to be the cofiber of
f
and
F
its source. This finishes the proof since
\mathrm{Ind}\mathbb{S}
is compact and satisfies
\Phi^{C_{p}}\mathrm{Ind}\mathbb{S}\simeq 0
.
∎

Remark 3.1.2
.

Unlike taking the appropriate connective covers, the procedure of tucking cannot be used in general to kill the homotopy groups of
X^{e}
. The reason is that the effect on the next higher homotopy group is quite brutal. However, if
X^{e}
is
(l-1)
-connected and
\pi_{l}X^{e}
is a finitely generated free
\mathbb{Z}[C_{p}]
-module, then the proof of
Lemma

3.1.1
shows that we can kill
\pi_{l}X^{e}
while making sure that the next higher homology group is unchanged. On the other hand, tucking preserves the geometric fixed points whereas the aforementioned connective covers do not.

Lemma 3.1.3
.

Let
Q
be a compact spectrum,
E
a
C_{p}
-spectrum and
f\colon Q\rightarrow\Phi^{C_{p}}E
a map.
Then there exists a compact
C_{p}
-spectrum
F
, a map
g\colon F\rightarrow E
, and an identification
\Phi^{C_{p}}F\simeq Q
under which
\Phi^{C_{p}}g=f
.

Proof.

First we reduce to the case where
E
is compact.
Write
E=\mathop{colim}\displaylimits_{i\in I}E_{i}
as a filtered colimit of compact
C_{p}
–spectra.
As
\Phi^{C_{p}}
commutes with colimits and
Q
is compact, there is some
i\in I
for which
f\colon Q\to\Phi^{C_{p}}E
factors through the compact spectrum
\Phi^{C_{p}}E_{i}
.
If now
F
is a compact
C_{p}
-spectrum together with a map
F\to E_{i}
which induces the map
Q\to\Phi^{C_{p}}E_{i}
on geometric fixed points, then the composite
F\to E_{i}\to E
satisfies the claim.

Now assume that
E
is compact.
Then there exists
k\in\mathbb{Z}
such that each map
Q\rightarrow T
to a
k
-connected spectrum
T
is nullhomotopic.
By
Lemma

3.1.1
, we can find
U\in\mathrm{Sp}_{C_{p}}
together with a map
E\rightarrow U
that has compact fiber and that induces an equivalence on geometric fixed points, such that
U^{e}
is
(k-1)
-connected. Thus,
\Sigma U^{e}
is
k
-connected, and consequently also
\Sigma U_{hC_{p}}
is
k
-connected. Note that
U
is compact as well.

Consider the following diagram where the lower horizontal maps form a fiber sequence and the square is cartesian by
2.2.3
and
2.2.5
.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{C_{p}}E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U^{C_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{C_{p}}U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U^{hC_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U^{tC_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma U_{hC_{p}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

The nullhomotopy of the long bent arrow induces the dashed morphism
a
, which in turn determines the dashed morphism
b
. By the adjunction from
Fact

2.2.2
, the map
b
is adjoint to a map

q\colon\infl_{C_{p}}^{e}Q\rightarrow U
which induces the map
Q\rightarrow\Phi^{C_{p}}U
on geometric fixed points. This fits into a fibre sequence
F\rightarrow E\rightarrow\cofib(q)
where the map
E\to\cofib(q)
is induced by the map
E\to U
from above.
Note that
F
is compact as
E
is compact,
U
is compact as observed above, and
\infl_{C_{p}}^{e}
preserves compactness.
On geometric fixed points, under the identification
\Phi^{C_{p}}E\xrightarrow{\simeq}\Phi^{C_{p}}U
, this gives

\Phi^{C_{p}}F\simeq\fib(\Phi^{C_{p}}E\rightarrow\cofib(Q\rightarrow\Phi^{C_{p}}E))\simeq Q

as desired.
Clearly, this identifies the map
\Phi^{C_{p}}F\rightarrow\Phi^{C_{p}}E
with
f
.
∎

Corollary 3.1.4
.

If
E
is a
C_{p}
-spectrum with
\Phi^{C_{p}}E
compact, then there exists a finite
C_{p}
-spectrum
F
and a map
g\colon F\rightarrow E

which induces an equivalence on geometric fixed points.

Proof.

Set
f=\mathrm{id}_{\Phi^{C_{p}}E}
in
Lemma

3.1.3
.
∎

The following lemma will be useful later.

Lemma 3.1.5
.

Consider a cospan
X\xrightarrow{f}Z\xleftarrow{g}Y
in
\mathrm{Sp}_{C_{p}}
where
f
and
g
induce equivalences on geometric fixed points. Additionally suppose
\Phi^{C_{p}}X\in\mathrm{Sp}
is compact. Then there exists a commutative square
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

with
F
compact such that all maps are equivalences on geometric fixed points.

Proof.

Let
E=X\times_{Z}Y
and find a fiber sequence
F\rightarrow E\rightarrow E^{\prime}

with
F
compact and
\Phi^{C_{p}}E^{\prime}\simeq 0
as provided by
Corollary

3.1.4
.
Then the outer quadrilateral in the diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}

has the desired properties.
∎

3.2
Compact and induced
C_{p}
-spectra

In this section we characterise compactness for
C_{p}
-spectra with trivial geometric fixed points through their underlying Borel
C_{p}
-spectrum.
Notice that
C_{p}
–spectra with vanishing geometric fixed points have the following crucial properties.

Lemma 3.2.1

(CoBorel compactness)
.

Let
X
be a
C_{p}
-spectrum with
\Phi^{C_{p}}X\simeq 0
. Then

(1)

for every
Y\in\mathrm{Sp}_{C_{p}}
the map
(-)^{e}\colon\map_{\mathrm{Sp}_{C_{p}}}(X,Y)\rightarrow\map_{\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}}}(X^{e},Y^{e})
is an equivalence;

(2)

the
C_{p}
-spectrum
X
is compact in
\mathrm{Sp}_{C_{p}}
if and only if
X^{e}
is compact in
\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}}
.

Proof.

We use the notations from
Fact

2.2.5
. Observe by
Fact

2.2.5
that
\Phi^{C_{p}}X\simeq 0
is equivalent to the condition that the adjunction counit
\beta_{!}\beta^{*}X\rightarrow X
is an equivalence. Now for (1), just note that

\map_{\mathrm{Sp}_{C_{p}}}(X,Y)\xleftarrow{\simeq}\map_{\mathrm{Sp}_{C_{p}}}(\beta_{!}\beta^{*}X,Y)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\map_{\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}}}(\beta^{*}X,\beta^{*}Y)
as claimed. For (2), note that
\beta_{!}\colon\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}_{C_{p}}
preserves and detects compactness as it is fully faithful and admits the colimit preserving right adjoint
\beta^{*}
.
This shows that
X^{e}=\beta^{*}X\in\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}}
is compact if and only if
X\simeq\beta_{!}\beta^{*}X\in\mathrm{Sp}_{C_{p}}
is compact.
∎

Notation 3.2.2
.

Following the notation from
[
NS18
, Def. I.3.7]
, we write
\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}}_{\mathrm{ind}}\subseteq\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}}
for the smallest idempotent–complete stable subcategory generated by the image of the functor
\mathrm{Ind}^{C_{p}}_{e}\colon\mathrm{Sp}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}}
. Similarly, we write
\mathrm{Sp}^{\mathrm{ind}}_{C_{p}}\subseteq\mathrm{Sp}_{C_{p}}
for the smallest idempotent–complete stable subcategory containing the image of
\mathrm{Ind}^{C_{p}}_{e}\colon\mathrm{Sp}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}_{C_{p}}
.
By
Lemma

3.2.1
(1), the functor
(\--)^{e}\colon\mathrm{Sp}_{C_{p}}\to\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}}
restricts to a fully faithful functor
\mathrm{Sp}^{\mathrm{ind}}_{C_{p}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}}_{\mathrm{ind}}
, which is also essentially surjective (and so is an equivalence) since
(-)^{e}=\beta^{*}
is essentially surjective and
\beta_{!}
and
\beta^{*}
are compatible with
\mathrm{Ind}^{C_{p}}_{e}
.

Lemma 3.2.3
.

As full subcategories of
\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}}
, we have the equality

(\mathrm{Sp}^{\omega})^{BC_{p}}\cap\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}}_{\mathrm{ind}}=(\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}})^{\omega}.

Thus, if
E\in\mathrm{Sp}_{C_{p}}
with
\Phi^{C_{p}}E=0
, then
E
is compact if and only if
E^{e}\in(\mathrm{Sp}^{\omega})^{BC_{p}}\cap\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}}_{\mathrm{ind}}
.

Proof.

The inclusion
(\mathrm{Sp}^{\omega})^{BC_{p}}\cap\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}}_{\mathrm{ind}}\supseteq(\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}})^{\omega}
is clear since
(\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}})^{\omega}
is generated under finite colimits and retracts by
\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}C_{p}/e\simeq\mathrm{Ind}^{C_{p}}_{e}\mathbb{S}
. For the converse, we use that

(\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}})^{\omega}\hookrightarrow(\mathrm{Sp}^{\omega})^{BC_{p}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{stmod}_{C_{p}}(\mathbb{S})\coloneqq(\mathrm{Sp}^{\omega})^{BC_{p}}/(\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}})^{\omega}

is a fibre sequence of small stable categories (c.f. for instance
[
CDH+23
, Lem. A.1.8]
) and that for
X,Y\in(\mathrm{Sp}^{\omega})^{BC_{p}}
we have the formula (c.f. for instance
[
Kra20
, Lem. 4.2]
)

\mapsp_{\mathrm{stmod}_{C_{p}}(\mathbb{S})}(X,Y)\simeq(Y\otimes DX)^{tC_{p}},

where
DX
is the pointwise Spanier–Whitehead dual in
(\mathrm{Sp}^{\omega})^{BC_{p}}
.

Observe that for any
X\in\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}}
and
Y\in\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}}_{\mathrm{ind}}
one has
\big(Y\otimes\mapsp(X,\mathbb{S})\big)^{tC_{p}}\simeq 0
owing to the fact that
(-)^{tC_{p}}
vanishes on
\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}}_{\mathrm{ind}}
and that
\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}}_{\mathrm{ind}}\subseteq\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}}
is a tensor–ideal. Therefore, for
Z\in(\mathrm{Sp}^{\omega})^{BC_{p}}\cap\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}}_{\mathrm{ind}}
, we see that

\mapsp_{\mathrm{stmod}_{C_{p}}(\mathbb{S})}(Z,Z)\simeq(Z\otimes DZ)^{tC_{p}}\simeq 0

and so
Z
is in the kernel of the functor
(\mathrm{Sp}^{\omega})^{BC_{p}}\rightarrow\mathrm{stmod}_{C_{p}}(\mathbb{S})
. Hence, by the fibre sequence above, we see that
Z\in(\mathrm{Sp}^{BC_{p}})^{\omega}
as required.

The statement about compact
C_{p}
-spectra follows by combining the first part with
Lemma

3.2.1
(2).
∎

3.3
Recognising
C_{p}
-Poincaré spaces

Construction 3.3.1

(Contravariant functoriality of dualising spectra)
.

Consider a map
f\colon\underline{{Y}}\to\underline{{X}}
in
\mathcal{S}_{C_{p}}^{\omega}
.
We explain how to construct a canonical “wrong–way” map

\mathrm{BC}^{f}\colon D_{\underline{X}}\longrightarrow f_{!}D_{\underline{Y}}.

(4)

Combining the contravariant functoriality of cohomology from
[
HKK24
, Construction 3.4.1]
with the defining property of the dualising spectrum, we obtain the natural transformation

X_{!}(D_{\underline{{X}}}\otimes-)\simeq X_{*}(-)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{BC}^{f}_{*}}Y_{*}f^{*}(\--)\simeq Y_{!}(D_{{\underline{{Y}}}}\otimes f^{*}(\--))\simeq X_{!}(f_{!}D_{{\underline{{Y}}}}\otimes-)

By the classification of colimit preserving functors, see
[
Cno23
, Corollary 2.30]
or
[
HKK24
, Theorem 2.1.37]
, this is induced by a map
\mathrm{BC}^{f}\colon D_{\underline{{X}}}\to f_{!}D_{{\underline{{Y}}}}
.

Now consider
\underline{X}\in\mathcal{S}_{C_{p}}^{\omega}
.
For the inclusion
\varepsilon\colon\underline{X^{C_{p}}}\rightarrow\underline{X}
of the singular part from
Construction

2.1.5
we thus obtain a map
\mathrm{BC}^{\varepsilon}\colon D_{\underline{X}}\rightarrow\varepsilon_{!}D_{\underline{X^{C_{p}}}}
.
Applying
\varepsilon^{*}
yields the map

\varepsilon^{*}\mathrm{BC}^{\varepsilon}\colon\varepsilon^{*}D_{\underline{X}}\longrightarrow\varepsilon^{*}\varepsilon_{!}D_{\underline{X^{C_{p}}}},

(5)

which may be viewed as a morphism in the nonparametrised functor category

to0.0pt{\hskip-0.46193pt\raisebox{0.19133pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip-0.35355pt\raisebox{0.35355pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{Sp}$}\hss}\hbox
